## SECTION A — CANONICAL EXECUTION CONTRACT

Only `KORA-WP-###` IDs defined in Section B of **this file** are executable. A coding prompt of the form `IMPLEMENT KORA-WP-XXX` must be resolvable from Section B alone — no reference to `90`, `93`, or `96` is ever required. Every package below carries all 32 mandatory fields, in fixed order, every time: **ID · Title · Milestone · Purpose · Pilot Status · Primary Closures · Early-Slice · Arch Sources · Code Truth · Existing Paths · Proposed New · Hard Deps · Conditional Deps · Parallelization · Data/Migration Impact · Expand/Migrate/Cutover/Contract · Service/API · Auth/RLS · UI · Privacy/Trust · Audit · ADMIN-020/Econ-Evidence · Async/Idempotency · Tests · Feature Flag · Rollback · Acceptance · Out of Scope · Size · Uncertainty · External Blockers · Evidence Gate.** A field with no applicable content is written `N/A — <reason>`, never omitted.

**DELTA ATOMIC UPDATE CONTRACT — binding, added 2026-09-23 (report `257`).** A DELTA may not be declared
COMPLETE until all three columns below hold. **Derived current truth must be regenerated or validated, never
manually assumed** — every registry inconsistency recorded in report `257` arose from a DELTA that updated the
sources and left a derived representation behind.

- **SOURCE — update:** Section B specifications · Section C hard edges · Sections D/E where applicable ·
  external blockers stated in the authoritative WP definition (including external legal gates).
- **DERIVED — regenerate, never hand-edit:** node counts · hard-edge counts · conditional-edge and
  scope-trigger counts · **Section M, recomputed from Section C** · the Section F topology count · the status
  aggregate where mechanically derivable.
- **VALIDATION — must hold:** Section B ↔ Section C exact node-set match · 0 cycles · 0 self-dependencies ·
  0 dangling references · 0 topological violations · migration-order consumers (Section AE) still consistent ·
  frozen or superseded execution-frontier material still carrying its supersession/clarification marker ·
  authority statements still valid.

These are mechanically checkable: `npm run governance:registry-check`. A DELTA whose checker run reports a
fail-closed failure is not complete. Known exceptions and not-derivable invariants are disclosed by that run
and are **not** cleared by it.

**FOUNDER REVIEW PUBLICATION CONTRACT — binding, added 2026-09-27 (report `274`, Founder ruling of the same
date).** A candidate that materially changes a **user-visible Product surface** may not be pushed for proof and
may not enter the canonical Product line until the Founder has reviewed that exact candidate and authorized
publication. **Exact-SHA CI is necessary technical evidence and is NEVER Founder publication authority.** This
restores the visual workflow evidenced by `KORA-WP-139`–`142` (reports `269`, `270`, `271`), in which the
candidate branch was held local and unpushed, the Founder ruling was recorded on the frozen candidate SHA, and
CI ran only afterwards.

- **BINDING SEQUENCE.** PRECHECK → IMPLEMENT LOCALLY → LOCAL CANDIDATE COMMIT → LOCAL TECHNICAL VALIDATION →
  FOUNDER REVIEW PACKAGE → **FOUNDER REVIEW / PUBLICATION DECISION** → *(only after approval)* PUSH PROOF
  BRANCH → EXACT-SHA CI → CANONICAL INTEGRATION → CANONICAL CI → GOVERNANCE COMPLETION where the WP contract
  is fully satisfied.
- **NO PRE-REVIEW PUSH.** For a materially user-visible candidate: do not push the candidate, do not push a
  proof branch, and do not make remote CI a prerequisite of Founder judgment. Local candidate commits and
  local technical validation are permitted and expected — they are the material the Founder reviews. The
  Founder reviews the exact frozen **local** candidate SHA.
- **MATERIAL EFFECT, NOT FILENAME.** Founder Review is mandatory where a change affects rendered UI,
  navigation/IA, visible labels, visible Product copy, visual hierarchy, typography, spacing, responsive
  composition, visible states, dashboards/cockpits, user-visible interaction flows, or design-system output.
  Test-only changes, evidence archives, governance artefacts, backend-only logic and **mechanically proven**
  non-rendering instrumentation do not independently trigger it. A path-based detector may flag candidates
  conservatively; **it may never mechanically waive Founder Review where a user-visible effect exists.**
- **POST-CI REMEDIATION.** If proof CI forces a visually material remediation, the resulting candidate returns
  to Founder Review before publication. If the remediation is **provably** non-visual, the prior publication
  authorization carries forward under the non-visual-descendant rule above.
- **THREE DISTINCT CONCEPTS, NEVER COLLAPSED.** *Technical Acceptance* is mechanical (tests, guards,
  validation, CI). *Founder Review* is the Founder seeing the implemented result and deciding whether that
  user-visible candidate may be published. *Formal Founder Visual Acceptance* is an immutable acceptance
  record required only where a WP or gate contract explicitly requires one. **Founder Review is mandatory for
  materially user-visible publication even where the WP requires no Formal Founder Visual Acceptance**, and it
  contributes nothing toward `KORA-WP-131`.
- **`KORA-WP-126` PREPARES REVIEW AND NEVER REPLACES IT.** Mechanical evidence cannot approve a visual change
  on the Founder's behalf. That package's own review-enforced / Founder-judgment boundary
  (`assertMechanisable()`) is binding here.
- **REVIEW PACKAGE.** Candidate SHA · baseline SHA · scope per WP · before/after evidence at the canonical
  viewport matrix, **shown to the Founder, not merely referenced** · affected personas and routes · measured
  warnings · outstanding acceptance debt · technical validation result · an explicit statement of what enters
  canonical if approved. The Founder is never sent to GitHub, CI, a terminal, the filesystem or artifact
  storage to reach the decision.
- **`INV-15`, when implemented, may verify only:** a Founder Review record exists; it names an immutable
  40-character candidate SHA; it declares the affected visual scope; an explicit publication authorization
  exists; and the pushed or proposed canonical candidate is the reviewed SHA or a provably non-visual
  descendant. It **must not** judge visual quality, infer Founder approval, approve screenshots, or infer
  approval from CI or from silence. **STATUS: PENDING ENFORCEMENT IMPLEMENTATION** — the governance harness
  currently exists only on the unpublished `gate3/prelive-privacy-remediation` line, and Gate 3 must NOT be
  published, nor unrelated Product/migration history pulled into canonical, merely to obtain the checker.
  **The contract above is binding regardless of enforcement availability.**
- **WAVE 4a IS A RECORDED PROCESS EXCEPTION.** PX-C Wave 4a (`KORA-WP-127`/`129`/`128`) reached canonical at
  `87fbdfa9621462f3d82e727726520fa590d2d4aa` **before** this gate existed. History is not rewritten and the
  publication is not silently reverted; **no retrospective Founder approval is inferred**. Founder judgment is
  recovered explicitly in report `274`. No package was marked COMPLETE by that wave.

FOUNDER VISUAL ACCEPTANCE PASSED.** Implemented on the accepted `KORA-WP-125` shared Product Experience foundation (`0bc14f6e484110ce65be8aa0c185a68208057359`) as a SIBLING of `KORA-WP-039`, `KORA-WP-048` and `KORA-WP-063`, never a descendant; completion commit `ddf3e9077ae68ff47937f49ddd46be060c768f1f`, worktree `/Users/simonefelicetti/KORA-wp012-worktree`, branch `feature/kora-wp-012-workforce-baseline-access-path`. **Canonical Founder ruling (2026-09-21), READING A — SURFACE:** the defect closed here is **UNDER-REACHABILITY OF A REAL ADMIN PRODUCT CAPABILITY**, not duplicate capability. **This row's own Acceptance sentence — "exactly one canonical `workforce-baseline` path remains reachable" — binds at the CAPABILITY-OWNERSHIP level, not at the URL level, and is NOT authority to delete a route.** Likewise this row's "Contract only (one duplicate route removed)", "Service/API: one route deprecated" and "UI: one page removed" were summary wording written without inspection of what the page uniquely renders; repository truth at execution time proved no duplicate existed — `/admin/companies/workforce-baseline` is the ONLY surface rendering baseline threshold validation and the N≥10 aggregate-group view, which neither `/admin/tenants` nor `CompanyWorkspacePanel` renders. The more specific canonical source governs and was followed: `46_GLOBAL_GAP_MATRIX`'s ADMIN-022 remediation ("Real page, no inbound nav (UNK-06); add a nav link or confirm out-of-band", risk "Real capability unreachable in-app", owner "product-owner decision"); the Founder chose **add a nav link**. Git evidence for the orphan: `f00346b` created the page AND its nav entry; `acb3a73` (B9.2 sidebar restructure) deleted the whole "Aziende Cliente" group and took the entry with it as a side effect — no decision ever retired the page. **Delivered:** one destination restored to the existing Companies group (`Workforce Baseline` → `/admin/companies/workforce-baseline`; the historical group was NOT recreated), a distinct rail glyph, its own route context, and — because reachability exposed a legacy interior — a WP-125 recomposition of that surface (PRIMARY validation verdict · SECONDARY company context · TERTIARY aggregate groups by dimension · supporting privacy rule and related paths), plus two correctness fixes: the visible dimension is now DERIVED from canonical data (the hard-coded singular `department` never matched the writer's plural `departments`, so the first render falsely reported no groups), and the known malformed stored label `organisazione` renders as `organizzazione` through a display-only, one-entry presentation-boundary correction. The stale cross-role link (`KORA Intake Engine™` → `/company/ingestion`) was retired from this surface in favour of the canonical Admin destination resolved from `ADMIN_NAV_GROUPS` (`Submission Queue` → `/admin/data-intake`); Company Registry retained. **Ownership map recorded:** `/admin/companies/workforce-baseline` = Admin inspection/validation · `/admin/tenants` = onboarding/write workflow · `/admin/companies/[companyId]/workspace` = per-company write workflow · `/company/workforce-baseline` = intentional SaaS-boundary notice (`f00346b`) · `app/api/admin/workforce-baseline/route.ts` = canonical shared API · `lib/live/workforce-baseline*` = canonical persistence path. **No legitimate workflow was removed and no route was deleted.** Data/Migration NONE holds unchanged (089 remains the highest migration); aggregate-only presentation, N≥10 suppression, both thresholds, KORA_ADMIN authorization, origin protections, GET/POST semantics, persistence and RLS are unchanged. Full record: report `237`.

**`KORA-WP-019`** — Opportunity — Standalone, Optional Object — I1 — Purpose: Opportunity exists and is usable but is **never** a mandatory gate before Commitment. Mechanically verified: zero packages in the 119-node registry (110 original + 9 Living KORAL) list `KORA-WP-019` as a hard dependency — Pilot Status: BASE PILOT NON-BLOCKER — Primary Closures: `CORE-011` — Early-Slice: N/A — Arch Sources: `68` §14 — Code Truth: ABSENT — Existing Paths: N/A — Proposed New: lightweight, mutable `opportunity` object — Hard Deps: `KORA-WP-014`, `KORA-WP-017` — Conditional Deps: N/A — Parallelization: fully independent of `020`+ — Data/Migration Impact: ADDITIVE — Expand/Migrate/Cutover/Contract: Expand only — Service/API: new lightweight service — Auth/RLS: Company-scoped — UI: minimal — Privacy/Trust: aggregate-safe — Audit: creation/conversion events — ADMIN-020/Econ-Evidence: N/A — Async/Idempotency: N/A — Tests: unit — Commitment can be created with a null Opportunity reference — Feature Flag: NO — Rollback: additive — Acceptance: both Commitment-creation paths (via Opportunity, direct) function identically — Out of Scope: any change to Commitment's schema making Opportunity mandatory — Size: S — Uncertainty: LOW — External Blockers: none — Evidence Gate: EV-P02 (Opportunity usefulness). — **SEQUENCING OVERLAY 2026-09-21: `NOT_YET_READY` — FOUNDER-DEFERRED (READING C). MECHANICAL STATUS UNCHANGED: `019` REMAINS `READY`.** This is a sequencing annotation about Founder priority, exactly in the form already used for the `124` and `125` overlays: it is **not** a fourth mechanical status, it is **not** `BLOCKED`, it adds **no** dependency edge, it changes no Hard Dep, Conditional Dep, milestone, acceptance criterion or scope trigger, and it does **not** reduce the READY count. **Founder Product ruling (2026-09-21):** Opportunity is intended as a meaningful **triage object** between a Need and a Commitment — Need → possible intervention worth evaluating → Opportunity → Commitment — and it must **never** become a mandatory gate. **The direct path `Need → Commitment` remains canonical and valid**, unchanged by this note and by this package's own Out of Scope. **Do NOT implement a minimal Opportunity shell now** (Reading A: a lightweight `Need + Context + Evidence` object) merely because `analytics.commitment.opportunity_id` is already reserved, the schema integration point exists, or a small service is technically buildable: the Founder's rationale is that such an object risks being an administrative intermediate with insufficient Product value, and the richer canonical concept becomes meaningful only once KORA can connect a Need with a plausible **capacity to respond** (which may include internal capacity, Advisor capability and/or Partner capability). That direction is **future Product direction, not an implementation specification, and no schema is frozen by it.** Canonical architecture already permits the deferral: `68` §9 lists Opportunity among the objects **not built for the first pilot** ("a Company may commit directly from a Need Hypothesis in cycle one; Opportunity is exercised from the second cycle or when an intervening triage step is actually used"), and this package is a BASE PILOT NON-BLOCKER. **No Partner scope is implied or activated:** `PARTNER-003` (`KORA-WP-056`) stays behind the INACTIVE "Partner delivery selected" trigger, and the Founder explicitly declined to activate it to make `019` executable. The reserved integration points stay intentionally **dormant and untouched**: `analytics.commitment.opportunity_id uuid CHECK (opportunity_id IS NULL)` (migration 065), its TypeScript mirror in `lib/commitment/commitment-service.ts`, and the `KORA-WP-020` guards. No `analytics.opportunity` table, service, API or UI exists or may be created under this deferral. **`019` remains the canonical owner of this future capability** — no replacement or successor package is created, nothing is renumbered, and resuming it requires an explicit Founder reconsideration. Two questions are recorded as open for that future resumption, not decided here: how Opportunity relates to Capacity semantics once that area exists, and disambiguation from the Product's three pre-existing uses of the word "opportunity" (`ActivationOpportunityService`, `/company/opportunities` + `/worker/opportunities`, `commons.post.category = 'opportunity'`) — all of which stay unchanged.

FOUNDER VISUAL ACCEPTANCE PASSED.** Canonical Founder ruling, **READING 1 — DECOMPOSE**: this package did not create a second Advisor Product; it completed the existing Advisor Portal by transforming the monolithic Advisor Company workspace into **one Advisor Portal + multi-Company portfolio + assignment-scoped Company workspaces + deep-linkable operational surfaces + WP124/WP125 Product Experience**. Canonical IA: `/advisor` (identity home) · `/advisor/companies` (portfolio of active assignments) · `/advisor/companies/[assignmentId]` with Panoramica, Messaggi, Appuntamenti, Note e riferimenti, Case, Valutazioni Review, KORAL Review. **The route key is `assignmentId`, not `companyId`** — the Advisor↔Company assignment is the authorization unit the existing APIs/services already use; no company→assignment translation layer was introduced. **Existing capability preserved 1:1** — the same eight write endpoints over the same ten existing Advisor APIs; no new business API, service or lib layer; authorization unchanged and server-side (`requireAdvisorUser` in `app/advisor/layout.tsx` + `callerAdvisorId` ownership checks, existing RLS), never moved to client-side filtering. Portfolio uses master/detail over existing assignment data with a single fetch — no per-Company N+1 fan-out. **DATA/MIGRATION: NONE** — verified at closure, highest migration remains 089. **The historical `Feature Flag: YES — full portal UX flagged per tenant during rollout` line in this row was intentionally NOT implemented, per explicit Founder ruling:** no canonical tenant-level Product entitlement architecture exists today (`FEATURE_FLAG_NAMES = [] as const`, zero consumers), and creating one here would enter the commercial-plan/feature-entitlement work the Founder has deferred until core KORA implementation is complete. No tenant feature flag, entitlement resolver, plan gating, `production_ready` misuse or commercial-package logic was introduced, and no Advisor SLA, pricing, compensation, billing or packaging. **This is a package-scope ruling, not a global registry-policy rewrite.** No Booking (`050`), Partner (`053`/`056`), Academy (`078`) or Living KORAL (`117`) scope was entered and no scope trigger was activated; `WP039` remains owner of Advisor governance/prerequisite/qualification workflow. Implemented on the accepted `KORA-WP-125` foundation (`0bc14f6e484110ce65be8aa0c185a68208057359`) as a SIBLING of `KORA-WP-039`, `048`, `063`, `012` and `049`, never a descendant; completion commit `55e123b7a093dcea8cac68ecf7f541be2cb250a2`, worktree `/Users/simonefelicetti/KORA-wp064-worktree`, branch `feature/kora-wp-064-full-advisor-portal`. Full record: report `239`.

Scope: incremental connectors (`KORA-WP-085`) — Size: M — Uncertainty: LOW — External Blockers: none — Evidence Gate: N/A. — **FOUNDER SEMANTIC RULING (2026-09-21) — READING 1: TENANT-SCOPED SESSION REUSE.** The historical wording *“reusable mapping across Companies”* (Purpose) and *“a saved mapping is reusable across Companies”* (Acceptance) are **NOT** the intended Product semantics and must not be read as cross-tenant sharing. **Corrected canonical acceptance: “a saved mapping is reusable across upload/mapping sessions for the same Company.”** Plain Product meaning: the second upload from the same Company is easier than the first. **Ownership = Company/tenant · Operational actor = KORA_ADMIN / KORA Operator · Reuse boundary = same Company only.** This ruling resolves the contradiction between this row's wording and the more specific frozen Product lineage (capability registry `45`, gap matrix `46`, cross-cutting `48`, Product Truth `FT-080`, `PD-028`, Arch Source `92` §19), in favour of that lineage. **Cross-Company templates, a global KORA mapping catalogue, mapping promotion and any Company-authored cross-tenant-readable object are explicitly OUT OF SCOPE** and would be a separate future capability, not created here. Unchanged by this ruling: status READY until closure, milestone I3, Hard Dep `KORA-WP-029`, no scope trigger, Feature Flag NO, NOT BASE PILOT SCOPE, additive/non-blocking nature. — **STATUS: COMPLETE (2026-09-22). FOUNDER VISUAL ACCEPTANCE PASSED.** Implemented under READING 1 — TENANT-SCOPED SESSION REUSE: same-Company reuse only, KORA_ADMIN operational actor, tenant-isolated persistence in the new `analytics.saved_column_mapping` (migration **090**, ADDITIVE/expand-only), user-selected **copy-on-use** application that pre-fills only matching headers and never mutates the stored mapping, **non-blocking** throughout (ingestion never requires or waits on a Saved Mapping). **A duplicate mapping name is an explicit conflict, never an overwrite** (Founder ruling 2026-09-22): the existing mapping is left unchanged and the Operator is told. **RLS least privilege** (Founder ruling 2026-09-22): a single KORA_ADMIN policy; no Company/Worker/Advisor/Partner read or write path, and no cross-tenant policy of any kind. PX-C remains PROVISIONAL, UNNUMBERED, NON-CANONICAL and NOT STARTED; no `KORA-WP-126` exists.**

**FOUNDER VISUAL QUALITY BAR — narrow Founder clarification, 2026-09-20. Canonical acceptance requirement of `KORA-WP-125`. This clarifies `125` only and changes nothing in `KORA-WP-124`.** The Founder-approved `KORA-WP-124` exemplars are **not loose inspiration**: they define the **minimum accepted visual-quality level for the real KORA Product**. `125` need not reproduce exemplar screens pixel-for-pixel where real Product semantics differ, but **the real Product must reach the same quality bar** — Product maturity, shell/navigation quality, visual hierarchy, information density, workspace composition, use of available viewport, typography, surfaces, controls, tables and forms, responsive behaviour, interaction and state quality, environment differentiation, and overall enterprise-software finish. **A technically correct implementation that still visibly resembles legacy KORA does NOT satisfy `125`. Green tests are necessary but NOT sufficient.**

**Closure evidence required by gate (K).** Before `125` may be marked mechanically COMPLETE, the **REAL KORA application** — real Next.js Product routes on local/synthetic data, never `KORA-WP-124` exemplars, never static mocks — must be captured at minimum at **1440px, 768px and 375px**, across enough representative authenticated environments/routes to prove the shared shell is genuinely common and not hard-coded to one page, and that evidence must demonstrate the common application foundation materially looks and behaves like the `124`-approved Product Experience. **`125` cannot close because tokens exist, components exist, the build passes, tests pass, or a new shell exists in code while real routes still look legacy.** Explicit Founder Visual Acceptance is required; "accepted in principle" is not sufficient.

**Pilot Status — FOUNDER-RATIFIED 2026-09-20.** `NOT BASE PILOT SCOPE` is the Founder's explicit ruling, not an inference. The field is closed; no correction is pending.

## SECTION B — ALL 131 FULL WP SPECIFICATIONS (PX-C — ENVIRONMENT EXPERIENCE REMEDIATION & VISUAL ACCEPTANCE)

Six packages, `KORA-WP-126`–`131`, canonically inserted per Founder authorization of 2026-09-22 (`KORA — CANONICAL ROADMAP REMEDIATION PASS`), on the evidence of report `241` and the accepted Audit-Gap / Canonical-Roadmap Reconciliation. **`PX-C` is the third and final Product Experience milestone**, completing the three-package structure report `210` §23 proposed and that this registry canonicalized only in part: `PX` (`124`, intent) and `PX-B` (`125`, shared foundation) were numbered; **PX-C was declined three times and left PROVISIONAL, UNNUMBERED and NON-CANONICAL.** The reconciliation established by census — not impression — that this single omission is the sole named owner of environment-wide body remediation and of the visual-acceptance instrument, and therefore of audit findings 16–21, 31, 64, 68 and 71.

**Why six packages and not one.** `210` §23's own reasoning against a single package applies unchanged: one node would have to contain an instrument build, four independent environment remediations and a final acceptance gate, giving it a non-atomic acceptance and an unresolvable Founder gate — the failure mode that left `117` open. Each package below has its own acceptance and its own evidence.

**Why these do not reopen `047`, `073`, `088`, `124` or `125`.** All five remain COMPLETE and are consumed, never reopened. `047`/`073`/`088` closed structural conformance; `124` froze intent; `125` built the shared foundation and is every PX-C package's Hard Dep. PX-C applies that foundation to page bodies, which `124`'s own Out of Scope ("environment-wide rollout") and `125`'s own scope deliberately excluded.

## SECTION B — ALL 137 FULL WP SPECIFICATIONS (RX — AUDIT REMEDIATION & AUDIT-ESTABLISHED OWNERSHIP)

Six packages, `KORA-WP-132`–`137`, canonically inserted per the same Founder authorization of 2026-09-22 and report `241`. **`RX` groups packages whose ownership was established by the 2026-09-22 Audit-Gap / Canonical-Roadmap Reconciliation**, which found 37 material findings with no canonical owner. `RX` is deliberately a provenance label, not a capability increment: filing these under `I3`–`I6` would misrepresent both them and those increments, exactly as Section AH-3 reasoned when separating `PX`. Each package carries its own sequencing overlay (pre-pilot, pre-paid-pilot, pre-production, post-pilot) recorded in Section AI.

**No package below reopens a COMPLETE WP.** Where a COMPLETE package proved insufficient for a new finding — `044` for the routing contract, `010`/`044` for FORCE RLS, `043` for CI enforcement — the remedy is a new node or a non-WP action, never a reinterpretation of closed work.

FOUNDER VISUAL ACCEPTANCE PASSED — WP139 TYPOGRAPHY SCOPE ONLY.** Branch `feature/kora-wp-139-typography-foundation`, worktree `/Users/simonefelicetti/KORA-wp139-worktree`, baseline `c46f2680cbd43b5e2c47c65b6688184fa8d109a7`, completion commit `cadb4178e943a774bab23d89c472623c22de7da6` (four commits: `c7057d7` scale and one family, `dc91f90` demonstrator migration, `a4ca40b` metric-hierarchy fix, `cadb417` shared-chrome floor fix). Exact-SHA CI: KORA CI #333, run `36195486621`, **attempt 2**, event `push`, branch `integration/wp139-ci-proof-2026-09-25`, head `cadb4178e943a774bab23d89c472623c22de7da6`, all four mandatory jobs green (RLS 329/329, R0-A 186/186, 0 skipped). Report `270`. **Delivered:** the canonical nine-role type scale as `TYPE` in the existing token file — the single numeric authority, transcribed from `docs/BENCHMARK_V2_CANONICAL_GRAMMAR.md` §5 and NOT from the WP-124 prototype, whose lower register sat below the Benchmark V2 bar (that prototype remains normative for colour, and the `PX` block is untouched); an 11px absolute floor with 11px reserved for `meta` alone, a 12px reading floor, and **body at 15px with no mobile variant**, because shrinking sustained reading text on the smaller screen is the opposite of the point; mobile variants only for the three roles that overflow a phone; nine typographic primitives with **no size, variant or weight prop**, so a caller states what text IS and the size follows. **One Product UI family:** Plus Jakarta Sans retained with weight 800 added for `display`; Instrument Serif and Playfair Display removed — both were loaded and rendered no text anywhere in the Product; Hanken Grotesk removed and its three consumers migrated. **No editorial family survives Wave 1.** On `PrivilegedAccessBanner` the family was partly a signalling device, so the signal moved onto the canonical `meta` role rather than being dropped, and `role="alert"`, `aria-live`, sticky placement, the pulsing dot and the per-variant colour are untouched; the `B168` assertion pinning `--font-hanken` was superseded in place with its intent preserved structurally. **Demonstrators:** `/company/reports` states roles rather than sizes with no hand-written size left, and `/advisor/companies/[assignmentId]` migrated across all seven tab entry points. **A correction this package made against its own earlier evidence:** the first (E) measurement used a static import-closure walk and reported zero, but `AppShell`, `Sidebar`, `Header` and `AccountMenu` are mounted by the LAYOUT and appear in no page's closure — the browser was rendering 22 sub-floor nodes on `/company/reports` and 6 on the Advisor surface. The floor is a property of the RENDERED surface, so the chrome was migrated in `cadb417` and **runtime re-measurement with real local sessions now reports 0 sub-floor nodes on both demonstrators**. A guard over the four chrome components prevents regression. **Enforcement at WARN**, selecting `Property[key.name='fontSize']` rather than a px regex, because 1,882 of ~2,600 inline decisions are bare numbers and a literal-only rule would miss 72% of the population; escalation to BLOCK is programme Definition of Done, owned by `KORA-WP-126`. **CI attempt 1 on this identical SHA failed one R0-A test with a PostgREST schema-cache error; it did not reproduce on attempt 2, no Product changed between attempts, and no root cause is claimed — it is recorded as NON-REPRODUCED, never as a WP139 regression.** **Founder ruling: this acceptance is WP139 TYPOGRAPHY SCOPE ONLY.** Explicitly NOT approved: `KORA-WP-141` composition, mobile information architecture, `KORA-WP-142` data storytelling, the overall Product Experience — which remains **NOT ACCEPTED AS FINAL QUALITY** — and **Benchmark V2 final visual acceptance, which is NOT granted.** Downstream observations recorded as inputs and not as WP139 defects: Advisor mobile tab compression and overall density/vertical length → `141`; raw qualification identifiers surfaced in the UI → later UX/content adjudication; data storytelling → `142`.

FOUNDER VISUAL ACCEPTANCE PASSED — WP140 SCOPE ONLY.** Implemented on branch `feature/kora-wp-140-surface-state-grammar`, worktree `/Users/simonefelicetti/KORA-wp140-worktree`, baseline `ac91cfa31e0bcad85071d467c9a5611a4668c29b`, completion commit `c46f2680cbd43b5e2c47c65b6688184fa8d109a7` (three commits: `ab0ce2c` grammar, `b070249` demonstrator adoption, `c46f268` acceptance hardening). Exact-SHA CI: KORA CI #330, run `36167556656`, event `push`, branch `integration/wp140-ci-proof-2026-09-25`, head `c46f2680cbd43b5e2c47c65b6688184fa8d109a7`, all four mandatory jobs green. Report `269`. **Delivered:** the nine surface roles and seven states as distinct typed components, each role documenting its use AND its non-use; the three semantic invariants enforced structurally rather than stylistically — `ZERO` and `NO DATA` carry non-substitutable prop shapes, and `SUPPRESSED` and `NOT YET AVAILABLE` differ from `ERROR` in ARIA role, live-region politeness and fault flag, so an assistive-technology user hears a boundary and a pending period as what they are; semantic-colour correctness made structural by removing every tone, variant and colour prop from the grammar — a caller states an `Assessment` and the treatment is derived, so a danger treatment on a healthy value has no entry point, and an alarm claim cannot be made without a stated reason; an unknown state throws instead of degrading to `ERROR`. **Privacy Boundary preserved by reuse, not rebuild:** `SUPPRESSED` delegates to `components/privacy/PrivacyBoundaryNotice.tsx`, which stays byte-identical to the baseline, as do `EmptyState`, `AccessDeniedState` and the shared token file — all four digest-asserted, which also keeps the `KORA-WP-139` collision surface clear. The `KORA-WP-047` accessibility lock was honoured, never superseded: `access-denied` still means an authorization failure, for which an alert role is correct. **Demonstrators:** `/company/reports` adopts seven roles where it previously had seventeen hand-rolled containers and no role, with three state corrections — the loading guard, an unscored period and KORA Contribution; `/advisor/companies/[assignmentId]` was recorded as conforming **by evidence, not mutation** (eight roles already adopted at baseline) and was deliberately not churned. **Founder ruling, recorded in scope: this acceptance is WP140 SCOPE ONLY and does NOT accept the current overall Product Experience, which remains NOT ACCEPTED as final quality.** The Founder's observed remaining deficiencies — typography too small and compressed, weak information hierarchy, excessive card and container density, insufficient visual storytelling, excessive vertical length especially on mobile, insufficient premium craft and originality, and important information not dominating quickly enough — belong primarily to `KORA-WP-139`, `KORA-WP-141` and `KORA-WP-142` and **must not be read as WP140 failures**. **Benchmark V2 final visual acceptance is NOT granted.**

### Product/Adoption (8)

| Evidence ID | Question / Hypothesis | Source | Category | Milestone | Blocking / Non-Blocking | Owner Capability | Collection Mechanism | Minimum Sample / Event Condition | Review Point | Decision Consequence | Current Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `EV-P01` | Do Companies actually move a Commitment from Draft to Committed unassisted? | Product Truth (`92` §"Commitment lifecycle"), DD-1 (Control Plane) | PRODUCT/ADOPTION | FIRST PILOT | Blocks the product claim "Commitment wedge validated"; does NOT block running the pilot itself | Product/Evidence Governance | Commitment record (`KORA-WP-022`'s commit transaction) + Operational Case linkage | At least one actual occurrence of a real pilot Commitment reaching `committed` without Admin hand-holding — NO FROZEN NUMERIC THRESHOLD beyond that single occurrence; a higher bar (e.g. N Companies) is a Founder/Economic decision not yet made | Pilot retrospective | Do not call the Commitment wedge validated until this occurs | OPEN |
| `EV-P02` | Is the optional Opportunity layer used when offered? | Product Truth; `KORA-WP-019`'s optionality design | PRODUCT/ADOPTION | POST-PILOT REVIEW | Non-blocking — informs prioritization only, never gates I1 | Product/Evidence Governance | Product event (Opportunity created/viewed events) | Usage rate observed across at least one pilot cohort — NO FROZEN NUMERIC THRESHOLD for a target usage rate | Pilot retrospective | Keep Opportunity optional; reconsider UX prioritization only — never make it mandatory | OPEN |
| `EV-P03` | Does the Investment×Needs pairing produce a decision a Company would act on? | Product Truth; DD-1 | PRODUCT/ADOPTION | FIRST PILOT | Blocks the claim that Investment×Needs is "sufficient to decide"; does NOT block pilot execution | Product/Evidence Governance | Commitment record review + Company Admin structured feedback | At least one pilot Investment reviewed and confirmed "sufficient to decide" by a real Company Admin | After each real Commitment | Do not claim Investment×Needs sufficiency validated until this occurs | OPEN |
| `EV-P04` | Does the hypothesis-to-evidence pipeline actually promote real needs? | Product Truth; `KORA-WP-017`/`023` | PRODUCT/ADOPTION | FIRST PILOT | Blocks the claim "Supported Need validated" | Product/Evidence Governance | Need Hypothesis / Supported Need state-transition record | At least one actual end-to-end conversion observed | Pilot retrospective | Do not claim Supported Need validated until this occurs | OPEN |
| `EV-P05` | Do Companies engage with Advisor review comments? | Product Truth; DD-2 (Advisor Operating Model) | PRODUCT/ADOPTION | FIRST PILOT | Non-blocking learning evidence — informs Advisor value proposition, does not block pilot | Advisor Governance | Operational Case + audit event (comment/response pairs) | At least one pilot review cycle with a Company-authored response to an Advisor comment | Pilot retrospective | Adjust Advisor engagement design if absent | OPEN |

## SECTION AC — LEGAL / COMMERCIAL / ACCOUNTING GATES

**Gate 2 (CTO/Architecture)** — CLOSED WITH CONDITIONS, staging only (`haqflkurpmeaxpikozjl`), 2026-06-22. Authorizes continued staging SQL/migrations for this registry's I0–I7 packages. Does **not** authorize production provisioning, production data, or real worker data for any `KORA-WP-###`.

**Gate 3 (Legal/DPO)** — OPEN. Blocks: live company data, real worker accounts, real HRIS/LMS integrations, production auth — i.e., blocks the **live/production activation** of any Base Company Pilot package (`001`–`049`), not their staging build. Directly blocks: `TRUST-01,02,03,05,06,07,08,14,15,16` (10 items, PRE-FIRST-PILOT, must close before real pilot data flows), and — on a later timeline — `TRUST-04,09,10,11,12,13,17,18` (8 items, PRE-REPEATABLE-SALES/PRE-SCALE). `KORA-WP-059` (Conditional Partner Legal Go-Live Gate) enforces `TRUST-04` specifically before any real Partner-delivery activity goes live, independent of `TRUST-04`'s own default timing. `KORA-WP-087` (Link Gate 3 Legal Review) enforces `TRUST-*` items specific to KORA Link before Link activation.

**Gate 5 (Tax/Fiscal)** — OPEN. Blocks: live fiscal/tax classification, automated guardrail enforcement, tax-advice outputs — i.e., blocks live activation of all I7 Prime packages (`100`–`109`; `110` is `DEFERRED`, not blocked-pending since it is not yet in scope at all). Directly linked: `PRIME-010` (principal-vs-agent conclusion), `PRIME-008` (provider selection), `PRIME-009` (fee-level market test). **`KORA-WP-121`–`123` (I7-PREP) inherit Gate 5's blocking transitively, through their own Hard Dep chain onto `101`** — never independently gated a second time; `KORA-WP-120` itself carries no Gate 5 exposure at all (zero Hard Deps, no Prime-financial content).

**Commercial decisions remaining (non-legal)**: `COMM-007` (Advisor economics model, Legal/Founder joint, blocks full closure of `074`/`075`); `GTM-04` (sales-org split decision, blocks `076`+ full org design); `PRIME-009`/`PRIME-012` (Prime fee/commission levels, Founder, block `104`/`108` full closure); `GTM-05`/`GTM-06`/`COMM-003`/`COMM-019`/`TERRITORY-004` (market-test evidence items, block nothing structurally but inform I3/I4/I6 commercial packages).

**Net effect on this registry**: zero `KORA-WP` package is blocked from **staging build** by any open gate **of the three evaluated in this section (Gates 2, 3 and 5)**. **CLARIFIED 2026-09-23:** that statement is scoped to those gates and is not a claim about every blocker of every kind — **`KORA-WP-069` is separately subject to external legal blocker `F-12`, which remains OPEN and bars it from starting at all** (see its own Section B row and `CLAUDE.md` §8). No other package is known to carry such a blocker. Gate 3/5 block only **live/production activation** of the specific packages named above — this distinction is load-bearing and must never be collapsed in any future coding prompt.

## SECTION AE — MIGRATION ORDER

Migration numbering continues strictly from the existing repository state (42 migrations, numbered `001`–`049` with gaps, most recent applied `027`/`028` per prior DD sessions). Every `KORA-WP` package whose Data/Migration Impact field (Section B) is not `NONE` produces migrations in the exact order given by Section M's single-thread topological order — a migration for a later-numbered `KORA-WP` is never applied before a migration for an earlier one it hard-depends on. All migrations produced under this registry are, per Gate 2's conditions, **staging-only** (`haqflkurpmeaxpikozjl`) until Gate 3 and Gate 5 both close for the specific package they belong to. No migration under this registry may drop or destructively alter any existing table; every schema change is additive-first (Expand), with Contract steps only for the explicitly-named dead/duplicated paths already identified in Section Y (`CORE-013b`, `COMPANY-008`, `COMPANY-017`, `MYKORA-003b`, `BOOKING-002`, `ADMIN-021`). The exact next migration number is assigned at implementation time of the first package that needs one (`KORA-WP-004`, Company Membership Table), continuing sequentially thereafter — this registry does not pre-assign migration numbers, since doing so before Gate 2's staging-only constraint is exercised package-by-package would itself be a forward-looking schema commitment beyond this task's mechanical-consolidation scope.

Canonically inserted per Founder authorization (source package: `.kora-audit/output/132_KORA_LIVING_KORAL_MASTER_PLAN_INSERTION_DESIGN.md`; supporting research `128`–`131`). Linear-plus-two-reuse dependency chain: `111` (true technical root) → `112` (also depends on `005`) → `113` → `114` → `115` → `116` (also depends on `007`, `031`) and `117` (parallel) → `118` → `119` (depends on both `117` and `118`). Zero existing package (`001`–`110`) was modified, and zero existing package now depends on any Living KORAL package — Living KORAL is purely downstream. Base Pilot (Section N, 40 IDs) and the Completion Frontier (Section K, 14 IDs) are both mechanically unaffected and unchanged — re-verified explicitly in their own sections above, not merely asserted. Priority note: `KORA-WP-111` is the only Living KORAL package that is mechanically `READY` today (a true technical root with no External Blocker or Scope Trigger); it and every other Living KORAL package nonetheless carry an explicit sequencing instruction — **do not begin any Living KORAL implementation before the current Base Pilot Blockers reach `COMPLETE`** — status and execution priority are deliberately different things here, per the Founder's own explicit instruction.

## SECTION AH-2 — PRIME PREPARATION CANONICAL INSERTION (this file's own delta over `133`)

Canonically inserted per Founder authorization (source deliverables, this same session: "KORA — UNIFIED INTELLIGENCE + KORA PRIME MASTER PLAN RECONCILIATION REPORT," "KORA — PRIME ROADMAP INSERTION DESIGN," and the Founder's own canonical-insertion-authorization prompt). Four new packages, `KORA-WP-120`–`123`, all `Pilot Status: NOT BASE PILOT SCOPE`, all `Milestone: I7-PREP` — a new milestone label, deliberately distinct from `I7` (never implying the same Gate-5/Program blanket gating `100`–`110` carry as a group).

**Why `I7-PREP` and not `I7` itself**: `I7`'s own Section B header states plainly, "All I7 packages are gated on Gate 5 closure + Founder Prime-launch decision (`84` §31), and on Program (`KORA-WP-060`)." That sentence is correct for `100`–`110` and is **not repeated or extended to `120`–`123`** by this insertion — `KORA-WP-120` has zero Hard Deps and zero External Blockers of its own; it is available for execution independent of Gate 5 or any Prime-launch decision. `KORA-WP-121`–`123` are gated, but only because they hard-depend on `101` (itself `I7`, itself Gate-5-gated) — a transitive, not a blanket, gating relationship. Collapsing `120`–`123` into `I7` proper would have silently and incorrectly implied `120` also waits on Gate 5, which is false and would have misled future execution-sequencing decisions.

**Why `KORA-WP-120` is a Hard Dependency of `KORA-WP-121`, decided explicitly rather than left implicit**: the base entitlement-balance model (`allocated`/`available`/.../`remaining`) does not itself require a reconciled taxonomy. The optional Pillar/Fiscal-Category/Action segmentation fields `121`'s own Purpose names as "required where canonically applicable" do. Per the Founder's own explicit preference in the roadmap-insertion-design authorization ("avoid building a Worker Entitlement schema against provisional classification vocabularies if it will later require Pillar/Fiscal/Action segmentation... if making 120 a hard dependency is semantically clean, do so"): it is semantically clean here, because building `121`'s segmentation fields against any of the three currently-fragmented vocabularies (Section B, `120`'s own entry) would force a breaking migration the moment reconciliation later completes — a cost this insertion chooses to avoid up front, at zero cost to `121`'s own start date, since `120` is independently READY today and can complete well before `101` (its other Hard Dep, `I7`/Gate-5-gated) ever does.

**Taxonomy as a hard gate for Prime-consuming capabilities, not merely advisory** (Founder Section 6 instruction, mechanically enforced in the DAG, not merely narrated): any future capability performing fiscal filtering, intervention-perimeter filtering, Partner Activity compatibility, Prime service classification, or Prime→Observation eligibility mapping inherits `120` as a real graph dependency — `121`, `122`, and `123` all do, directly; `KORA-WP-060`'s own clarified acceptance criteria (below) explicitly cannot be considered complete for its Prime-relevant clauses before `120` completes, stated in `060`'s own entry rather than invented as a new graph edge onto an already-COMPLETE-eligible package's Hard Deps (see the next paragraph for why `060`'s own Hard Deps field is not touched).

## SECTION AH-3 — PRODUCT EXPERIENCE CANONICAL INSERTION (this file's own delta over `142`)

Canonically inserted per Founder authorization (source deliverables, this same session: report `210` full 123-WP DAG + Product Experience / Visual Excellence audit; report `217` Formal Consolidation Audit post WP-016, verdict B, accepted; report `218` PX-A canonicalization design, accepted; and the Founder's own new-registry-version authorization prompt). **One** new package, `KORA-WP-124`, `Pilot Status: NOT BASE PILOT SCOPE`, `Milestone: PX` — a new milestone label, deliberately distinct from every existing increment.

**Why a new package at all.** Reports `210` and `217` established by field census over all 123 existing packages — not by impression — that the roadmap has **no whole-platform owner** for Product Experience design, visual/art direction, typography, density, surface hierarchy, interaction character, iconography, motion, data-visualisation language, in-product brand expression, or visual quality acceptance. Of 32 audited experience dimensions: **3 COVERED (baseline-only, all from `088`), 6 PARTIALLY COVERED, 23 NOT COVERED.** Across all 123 specifications there are **zero** occurrences of typography, iconography, illustration, empty states, success/confirmation states, data-visualisation, hover/focus state design, motion grammar, microcopy/content design, perceived performance, visual regression, usability validation, or Founder visual acceptance. Of 40 packages with real UI scope, **none** classifies as full Product-Experience work. The gap is structural, not incidental.

**Why `PX` and not an existing increment.** Every existing milestone (`I0`–`I7`, `I7-PREP`, the Conditional extensions, `LIVING KORAL`) labels a **feature or capability increment**. `KORA-WP-124` delivers **intent and an acceptance contract**, consumed by later implementation rather than shipping a user-facing capability of its own. Filing it under any existing increment would misrepresent both it and that increment. `PX` therefore stands apart, exactly as `I7-PREP` was separated from `I7` and `LIVING KORAL` from the numbered increments.

**Why `124`.** `142`'s namespace is contiguous `001`–`123` — 123 specifications, no gaps, no duplicates, **no unused or reserved slot**. Both prior roadmap extensions used the next sequential numbers (Living KORAL `111`–`119`; Prime Preparation `120`–`123`), each via a new registry version superseding its predecessor rather than an in-place edit. `124` is the only canonically consistent number, and this file is the only canonically consistent mechanism.

**What `124` does NOT do.** It is not a frontend rewrite, not environment-wide remediation, not page-by-page polishing, and not a replacement for future PX-B/PX-C work. It does **not** reopen `KORA-WP-047`, `073` or `088` — all three remain COMPLETE and are its Hard Deps precisely because it builds on them. It does **not** reopen Living KORAL: `KORA-WP-117` remains OPEN / Founder-deferred / NOT COMPLETE, `118`/`119` remain behind it, and `124` may define only how Living KORAL eventually **coexists** with the broader product language — never renderer work, never Round 6, never Package B, never a `BoundedKoralGeometry` change.

**Relationship to future PX work, conceptually canonical but not itself canonicalized here:** `KORA-WP-124` (PX-A) defines and freezes Product Experience intent → a future PX-B translates that frozen intent into reusable Product Experience system primitives → a future PX-C applies and remediates whole environments and performs final Product Experience / Visual Acceptance. ~~**PX-B and PX-C remain PROVISIONAL, UNNUMBERED, NON-CANONICAL and NOT STARTED; their detailed acceptance criteria are deliberately not frozen by this registry version**~~ — **SUPERSEDED 2026-09-20 (DELTA 2) for PX-B ONLY: PX-B is now canonicalized as `KORA-WP-125`, and its acceptance criteria ARE frozen, in Section B (PX-B) — precisely because `124`'s own output (the Gate I artefacts) plus `KORA-WP-039`'s real-Product evidence now define them. PX-C remains PROVISIONAL, UNNUMBERED, NON-CANONICAL and NOT STARTED; its acceptance criteria remain deliberately unfrozen and will be defined after `125` supplies implementation truth. No `KORA-WP-126` is created.** The original wording is preserved above as the state at DELTA 1.

**Strategic sequencing annotation (NOT a mechanical status, NOT an overlay change).** The Founder has recorded that the following existing READY packages, all carrying material UI scope, are **strategically deferred until `KORA-WP-124` has established Product Experience direction**: `039`, `048`, `063`, `064`, `066`, `068`, `069`, `070`, `071`, `072`, `074`, `075`, `076`, `077`, `085`, `086`, `089`, `094`, `095`, `098`, `099`. **Their mechanical Registry status is unchanged — they remain READY, are NOT marked BLOCKED, and `NOT_YET_READY` is NOT repurposed to record this.** This is a sequencing note about Founder priority, nothing more, and it confers no dependency edge. **UPDATE 2026-09-20: `KORA-WP-124` is COMPLETE and Gate I is MET, so this strategic deferral is SATISFIED for all 21 packages. Their mechanical statuses remain unchanged, none is activated, none is marked COMPLETE, and no dependency edge is added by this update. `KORA-WP-085` retains its independent `External Blockers: Gate 3` condition.**

**SECOND SEQUENCING OVERLAY — 2026-09-20, DELTA 2, evidence-based (NOT a mechanical status, NOT a hard edge).** The `124` deferral above is SATISFIED and stays satisfied. A **new and separate** overlay now replaces it for the same 21 packages:

> **`WP125 Product Experience Foundation must precede further UI-bearing implementation.`**

Affected packages, unchanged in number and membership: `039`, `048`, `063`, `064`, `066`, `068`, `069`, `070`, `071`, `072`, `074`, `075`, `076`, `077`, `085`, `086`, `089`, `094`, `095`, `098`, `099`. **Their mechanical Registry statuses are unchanged — they remain exactly as recorded, none is marked BLOCKED, and `NOT_YET_READY` is NOT repurposed to record this. No hard-dependency edge to `125` is added to any of the 21 historical package definitions; the only new edge in DELTA 2 is `124→125`.**

**Why this overlay is evidence-based rather than precautionary.** `KORA-WP-124` satisfied the first overlay by freezing intent, but `KORA-WP-039` — the first real Product implementation against that frozen intent, built with full Founder oversight — measured what happens with no shared implementation layer: a route-local stylesheet duplicating shell-level surface/table/form/state grammar; a route-local colour-token adapter; a named local exemption inside the completed `KORA-WP-088` presentation-colour guard; page-level responsive behaviour keyed to viewport width while the real constraint is container width; and two visual defects (`D2`, and the container width driving `D1`) that are not fixable from inside a page at all. Multiplied across 21 packages this yields up to 21 local stylesheets, 21 token adapters, 21 guard exemptions and 21 disagreeing responsive models — all of which `125` would then require to be rewritten. The foundation is built once either way; built first it is consumed 21 times, built last it forces 21 rewrites.

**`KORA-WP-085` continues to retain its independent `External Blockers: Gate 3 OPEN` condition**, which is unaffected by, and not merged into, either overlay.

**`KORA-WP-039` multidimensional disposition, recorded explicitly so no dimension hides another (mirroring the `085` precedent below):** mechanical status **unchanged** — the Registry schema needs no new representation, and none is invented; Founder-activated and implemented up to, but deliberately not including, local commit; **IMPLEMENTATION PAUSED BEFORE COMMIT (2026-09-20)** by Founder decision following the real-Product visual review; work retained and NOT discarded — prerequisite-eligibility capability, Admin API, auth, audit chain, `KORA-WP-031` validity reuse, focused tests, full regression, all verified green; its worktree remains untouched at baseline `84128e81b0bb5a617bb7c3ac7802d1a5491c2a84`, nothing staged, nothing committed, nothing pushed. After `125` completes, `039` is expected to resume from the `125` baseline, retain its functional implementation, replace its local Product Experience CSS and token adapter with the shared `125` foundation, remove its temporary local-colour exemption from the `088` guard, fix its page-level composition defects `D3`/`D4`, and revalidate `D1`/`D2`/`D5` against the shared foundation. **None of that is performed or authorized by this registry update.**

**`KORA-WP-085` multidimensional status, recorded explicitly so no dimension hides another:** mechanical status **READY** (numbered Hard Deps `016` and `028` both COMPLETE, unmet set empty); **External Blocker: Gate 3 OPEN**; strategically deferred pending `124` because it carries connector-config UI. It is neither moved to mechanical BLOCKED for Gate 3, nor labelled `NOT_YET_READY` to disguise the external blocker.

**Activation — SUPERSEDED 2026-09-20: `KORA-WP-124` was activated, implemented and COMPLETED; Gate I MET (explicit Founder Visual Acceptance, 2026-09-20; report `231`). The paragraph below is preserved as the state at insertion time.** Insertion into this registry is **not** activation. `KORA-WP-124` is **canonicalized, not started**: no implementation artifact, no visual direction, no mockup, no design round, and no Founder visual adjudication has been produced or begun. Its Hard Deps are already satisfied (`047`, `073`, `088` all COMPLETE), so under the existing status model it is **mechanically READY and canonically available** — which is a statement about availability, not about having been activated. No new mechanical status is invented to express this.

## SECTION AH-4 — SHARED PRODUCT EXPERIENCE FOUNDATION CANONICAL INSERTION (this file's DELTA 2)

Canonically inserted per Founder authorization of 2026-09-20 (`KORA — FOUNDER GOVERNANCE DECISION: CANONICALIZE PRODUCT EXPERIENCE FOUNDATION AS KORA-WP-125`), on the evidence of report `232` (Product Experience implementation gap, established by reading this registry rather than by assumption) and report `231` (`KORA-WP-124` canonical completion, Gate I MET). **One** new package, `KORA-WP-125`, `Pilot Status: NOT BASE PILOT SCOPE`, `Milestone: PX-B` — a new milestone label, deliberately distinct from `PX` because `PX` is intent and `PX-B` is implementation.

**Why a new package at all.** This registry canonicalized the intent layer and, in three separate places, explicitly declined to canonicalize its implementation: the DELTA 1 preamble ("No PX-B or PX-C package is introduced, numbered or canonicalized by this file"), the `PX` Section B preamble, and Section AH-3's future-PX paragraph ("PX-B and PX-C remain PROVISIONAL, UNNUMBERED, NON-CANONICAL and NOT STARTED"). `KORA-WP-124`'s own Code Truth field states the gap outright: "no canonical package owns whole-platform Product Experience or visual/art direction today; `047`/`073`/`088` closed structural conformance only." Its `UI` field limits it to "specification plus representative exemplars — NOT as environment-wide implementation", and its Out of Scope names "full component-system implementation", "environment-wide rollout" and "PX-B implementation" explicitly. No package between `124` and the 21 UI-bearing packages existed. `125` closes exactly that gap and nothing wider.

**Why not reopen `047`, `073` or `088`.** All three remain COMPLETE and are `124`'s Hard Deps. `047` is a pilot-slice accessibility/design/IA subset ("Out of Scope: full site-wide closure"). `073` is site-wide **accessibility and IA** closure, whose semantics `125` is explicitly forbidden to change. `088` is site-wide **structural** responsive/design closure against the legacy visual system — it is the guard that correctly caught `KORA-WP-039`'s local colour adapter, and it is to be preserved and strengthened by `125`, never relaxed to accommodate it. None of the three owns visual language, shell presentation or component primitives, and none is reopened here.

**Why `125`.** The namespace was contiguous `001`–`124` with no gap, no duplicate and no reserved slot. Every prior extension used the next sequential number (Living KORAL `111`–`119`; Prime Preparation `120`–`123`; Product Experience `124`). `125` is appended, never inserted.

**What `125` does NOT do.** It is not PX-C, not environment-wide remediation, not page-by-page migration, and not completion of the 21 UI-bearing packages. It does not change business logic, domain models, the data model, auth, or `073`'s IA and navigation semantics — it may change navigation PRESENTATION only. It does not touch Gate 3, Supabase remote, Vercel Production or Production rollout, and it introduces no DB migration. It does not redesign the Founder-approved `124` direction; it implements it. It does not reopen Living KORAL: **`KORA-WP-117` remains OPEN / Founder-deferred / NOT COMPLETE**, with `118`/`119` behind it, unchanged by this delta.

**The one acceptance criterion that makes this package real.** Gate (J) in Section B: the shared foundation must be APPLIED to the real common application shell. `125` cannot be closed by a component library that exists in a folder while the real Product looks unchanged. That criterion exists because the Founder's stated target is "KORA real Product now visibly has the new common Product foundation", not "new-looking cards inside the old KORA shell".

**Recommended baseline, verified from dependency reality, not assumed:** `84128e81b0bb5a617bb7c3ac7802d1a5491c2a84` — it carries the 42 Gate I-accepted `design/wp124-final/` artefacts; the frozen RC `5f9426974791b6e2ff292aa7634860c4666bd91a` is an ancestor of it; the Gate 3 commit `0cdc7e0dd1c9293b25a84bb921942cc792052427` is **NOT** an ancestor of it; and it is exactly RC + one commit with no unrelated drift. `125` has no dependency requiring any Gate 3 commit. Gate 3 remains isolated on `gate3/prelive-privacy-remediation @ 0cdc7e0d…`; PR #172 remains frozen and unmerged at `5f942697…`.

**Unresolved items this delta deliberately does NOT touch, so none is silently closed by it:** the `KORA-WP-120` registry inconsistency remains open and untouched; `KORA-WP-117`'s Founder deferral remains in force; the `CLAUDE.md` §6 multi-record Admin interpretation (a full KORA Index surface satisfies §6, but a cross-tenant list showing an Index per row cannot carry a ten-component breakdown per row) remains an open governance interpretation, recorded in `design/wp124-final/HANDOFF.md` §22 as escalate-never-invent, and is neither resolved nor pre-judged by `125`.

**Closure — FOUNDER VISUAL ACCEPTANCE GRANTED 2026-09-21; `KORA-WP-125` is COMPLETE.** Gate (K) PASSED on personal Founder review of the real Product across all five representative surfaces (Admin, Company, Worker, Partner, Advisor). PX-C remains PROVISIONAL, UNNUMBERED, NON-CANONICAL and NOT STARTED; no `KORA-WP-126` is created by this closure. Full record: report `233`.

## SECTION AI — NON-WP REMEDIATION / RELEASE-GOVERNANCE TRACK (`R0`) — added 2026-09-22, execution-tracking schema added 2026-09-22 (patch)

**These are NOT Work Packages.** They exist because the 2026-09-22 reconciliation found material audit
gaps that are **release-governance or CI actions, not Product implementation** — creating Product WPs for
them would be a category error. They are nonetheless **mechanically auditable execution items** with their
own state vocabulary.

### AI.1 — SEPARATION RULE (binding)

`R0` items **DO NOT**:
- count as Registry WP nodes;
- affect the `COMPLETE` / `READY` / `BLOCKED` WP counts;
- appear in Section C, create hard edges, or become graph nodes;
- receive a WP number.

`R0` items **DO**:
- require evidence before reaching `R0_COMPLETE`;
- participate in the pilot and release gates of Section AJ;
- require a completion record in the `.kora-audit/output` corpus;
- remain visible in this section until closed.

**State vocabulary is deliberately distinct.** `R0` items use **`R0_OPEN` / `R0_BLOCKED` / `R0_COMPLETE`**,
never the bare words `READY`/`BLOCKED`/`COMPLETE`, so that an `R0` state can never be mistaken for, or
summed into, a WP graph count. The WP aggregate in Section F is computed over Section C nodes only and is
structurally incapable of including these items.

### AI.2 — EXECUTION SCHEMA

Every `R0` item carries exactly these fields: **ID · Title · Execution domain (owner type) · State ·
Preconditions · Acceptance · Required evidence · Gate affected · Completion record.**

| ID | Title | Execution domain | State | Preconditions | Gate affected |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **R0-A** | CI real-DB enforcement | CI / build engineering | **`R0_COMPLETE`** (2026-09-22, report `245`, commit `ec0ff4546fdf8bc06f3b76e59f66a1b4ebaa1528`) | none | CONTROLLED PILOT |
| **R0-B** | Canonical deploy-line declaration | Release governance (Founder-authorized) | **`R0_COMPLETE`** (2026-09-22, report `243`) | none | CONTROLLED PILOT |
| **R0-C** | Integrated staging validation | Release governance + QA | **`R0_COMPLETE`** (2026-09-23, report `256`, exact-SHA `1e981f8067c388fc14ed3ea44f650cf2cf763a39`) | `R0-B` = `R0_COMPLETE` — **SATISFIED**; remote-CI — **SATISFIED**; staging designation — **SATISFIED** | CONTROLLED PILOT |
| **R0-D** | Governance publication / auditability | Governance | **`R0_COMPLETE`** (2026-09-23, report `259`, release `KORA-GOVERNANCE-v2`, remote commit `2138a48ca1edcb00820b92b29c01bfacf1232464`) | none | PAID EXTERNAL PILOT |

**Model B**, in which Production is deployed from an immutable release-candidate SHA and **`main` is an

## SECTION AK — METHODOLOGY / CALIBRATION OWNERSHIP — added 2026-09-22

Recorded, not newly created. Audit findings 12, 13, 14 have **no WP owner by design**: this registry
contains zero occurrences of "Delphi" or "empirical", because calibration is governed **constitutionally**
— CLAUDE.md §6: *"Not empirically validated — Delphi Study calibration is post-pilot."* Evidence
collection during the pilot is already owned by the **17 `EV-*` Evidence Gates** distributed across
Section B; those are referenced here, never duplicated. **Pre-empirical status is therefore an accepted,
planned pre-pilot condition — not a roadmap failure — and becomes a Production-gate decision point
(Section AJ), not a pre-pilot blocker**, provided the non-suppressible disclosure labels ship.
**No pre-pilot empirical-calibration WP is created.** New methodology work is warranted only if a real
ownership gap remains after pilot evidence collection.

(`132`–`137`).

**Why new packages rather than reopening COMPLETE work.** The reconciliation found 37 material findings
with no canonical owner. In every case the plausible owner was already COMPLETE with unrelated
acceptance — `047`/`073`/`088` closed conformance, `044` closed route validation, `010` closed base RLS,
`043` closed the test pyramid. Per CLAUDE.md §8 and this registry's own convention, a COMPLETE package is
not reinterpreted to absorb a later finding; the remedy is an acceptance extension to a READY package
(`070`, `072` — both minimal, both bounded) or a new node.

**Why twelve and not thirty-seven.** Grouped by root cause into eight clusters, of which six needed
nodes. `PX-C`'s six-way split is mandated by `210` §23's own atomicity reasoning, not by preference.

**Why `126`–`137`.** The namespace was contiguous `001`–`125` with **no gap, no duplicate and no
reserved slot** — verified mechanically before assignment. The only prior reference to `126` was the
negative declaration in the `PX-B` preamble (*"no `KORA-WP-126` exists"*), which asserted its freedom and
anticipated exactly this use; that sentence is struck and superseded in place, not deleted. Every prior
extension appended sequentially (Living KORAL `111`–`119`; Prime Preparation `120`–`123`; PX `124`;
PX-B `125`); `126`–`137` continues that convention.

precede decomposition would compound the debt `133` exists to remove.

## SECTION AL — EXECUTION STATUS LEDGER — added 2026-09-24 (report `265`) — closes `INV-08`

**This section is the canonical source of mechanical status.** It exists because the 32-field Section B
schema carries `Pilot Status` (scope) but **no mechanical status field**, which left `INV-08`
`NOT_DERIVABLE`. Historical statuses were **not fabricated**: they were **derived**, and the derivation is
recorded here so any future session reproduces it rather than re-guessing.

### AL.1 — Canonical derivation rule

```
COMPLETE  := recorded in AL.2 below
READY     := ¬COMPLETE ∧ every Hard Dep (Section C) ∈ COMPLETE
BLOCKED   := ¬COMPLETE ∧ ∃ Hard Dep ∉ COMPLETE
```

**`READY` and `BLOCKED` are never written down — they are computed from Section C and AL.2.** A scope
trigger, an external blocker and a Founder deferral are **execution-eligibility overlays**, never mechanical
statuses: `KORA-WP-019` and `KORA-WP-117` are both mechanically `READY` **and** Founder-deferred, exactly as
this registry already stated before this section existed.

### AL.2 — COMPLETE set (70)

`001, 002, 003, 004, 005, 006, 007, 008, 009, 010, 011, 012, 013, 014, 015, 016, 017, 018, 020, 021, 022,
023, 024, 025, 026, 027, 028, 029, 030, 031, 032, 033, 034, 035, 036, 037, 038, 039, 040, 041, 042, 043,
044, 045, 046, 047, 048, 049, 062, 063, 064, 066, 073, 088, 111, 112, 113, 114, 115, 116, 120, 124, 125,
126, 132, 138, 139, 140, 141, 142`

### AL.3 — Evidence basis, and its honest limits

A package is in AL.2 when it carries **completion evidence** at canonical Product SHA
`6bc22f5bae93270527b12f5b9d15b7997098db75`:

| Evidence class | Packages |
|---|---|
| Dedicated test suite `tests/**/kora-wp-NNN-*.ts` | 60 suites present, enumerated mechanically from the repository |
| Founder Visual Acceptance recorded in this registry | `124` (report `231`), `125` (report `233`) |
| Integration-level coverage rather than a per-WP unit file | `043` (14-node Completion Frontier), `045` (first-pilot E2E scenario) |
| Ancestor closure — a COMPLETE package's Hard Deps are COMPLETE by construction | **only `001` and `010`**, both independently corroborated by the `R0-A` CI real-database gate and the RLS-03/05/06 enforcement suite |
| Exact-SHA canonical CI on a pushed ref | **`138`** — KORA CI #327, run `36066886221`, event `push`, head `ac91cfa31e0bcad85071d467c9a5611a4668c29b`, attempt 2, all four mandatory jobs green; report `267` |
| Exact-SHA CI on a pushed proof ref **plus** Founder Visual Acceptance | **`140`** — KORA CI #330, run `36167556656`, event `push`, head `c46f2680cbd43b5e2c47c65b6688184fa8d109a7`, attempt 1, all four mandatory jobs green; Founder Visual Acceptance granted **for WP140 scope only**; report `269` |
| Exact-SHA CI on a pushed proof ref **plus** Founder Visual Acceptance | **`139`** — KORA CI #333, run `36195486621`, event `push`, head `cadb4178e943a774bab23d89c472623c22de7da6`, **attempt 2** (attempt 1's single R0-A failure did not reproduce on the identical SHA; no Product changed between attempts and no root cause is claimed), all four mandatory jobs green; Founder Visual Acceptance granted **for WP139 typography scope only**; report `270` |
| Exact-SHA canonical CI on a pushed ref **plus** the instrument's own self-test | **`126`** — KORA CI #345, run `36277974910`, event `push`, head `81d3cf3f9158017659a80429c1c3a522ca769f7d`, attempt 1, all four mandatory jobs green; proof run #344 (`36277799697`) on `integration/wp126-ci-proof-2026-09-27` at the same SHA. 62-case instrument self-test including the required viewport-matrix-vs-`PX_BREAKPOINTS` guard; report `273`. **No Founder visual acceptance was sought or recorded — WP126 introduces no Product surface** |
| **Excluded despite a test file** | **`117`** — Founder-deferred; its suite exists as uncommitted work and the package is **not** COMPLETE |

**Corroboration, stated as corroboration and not as proof.** This set was derived from code truth **before**
being compared with the declared aggregate, and it reproduced it exactly at that delta — COMPLETE 64 · READY 35 ·
BLOCKED 39 · TOTAL 138, the aggregate current on 2026-09-24. Deliberately left unbolded: this is a
historical corroboration record, not a live declaration, and the checker reads a bolded total as current truth. It also independently places `019` and `117` in `READY`, which is what this
registry separately asserts about both. **A search over the dependency graph and the declared aggregate alone
returns more than 600 candidate sets, so the aggregate does NOT uniquely determine membership** — AL.2 is
therefore authoritative *because of its evidence basis*, not because it fits the counts. Any future
correction to AL.2 must come from completion evidence, never from re-fitting the totals.

its external conditions hold.

### AM.0 — PRODUCT EXPERIENCE DELTA — added 2026-09-25 (DELTA 5, report `266`)

The ratified Completion Program is **not rewritten**; this records the minimum change Product Experience introduces.

**Packages added:** `139`–`143` (PX-D) and `144` (I-PX). **Amended:** `126`, `127`, `128`, `129`, `131`; `130`
referenced, contract unchanged. **Benchmark V2 is the quality standard** for all Product Experience acceptance
(Section AN).

**Milestone impact.** **M2 Core Product Correctness** gains no requirement — it remains `138` + `134`. **M3 Paid
External Pilot Ready** is unchanged: `131` still gates it, but now on Benchmark V2 Gates A–I rather than the
`124`/`125` bar, so **the requirement is raised without adding a package to the gate**. **M8 Full Product Experience
Ready** now means `139`–`143` plus `126`–`131` under Gates A–I. **M10 Founder Target Platform** additionally requires
**`144`**, because the Product cannot answer *"what changed?"* without it.

**No gate contract is amended by this delta.** `144` explicitly does **not** block the Controlled Pilot, the Paid
External Pilot or Production.

**Critical-path impact.** The Product Experience path is `139`‖`140` → `141`‖`142` → `126` → persona → `131`. Its
practical constraint is **not** that graph: it is (a) `138` clearing the five Company surfaces first, (b) `141` as the
long pole at XL / MEDIUM-HIGH risk, and (c) Founder Visual Acceptance as a serialising human step at W1
demonstrators, each persona and `131`. **Partner Product Experience is not on this path at all.**

**Roadmap state after this delta: 144 packages — COMPLETE 64 · READY 37 · BLOCKED 43.** Remaining work is 80
packages, of which six are the Product Experience additions recorded here.

### AM.1 — CAPABILITY-BEFORE-WRITE — binding sequencing invariant

**No package may introduce a governed Partner write or action on the basis of `role === PARTNER` alone.**
Any package introducing a Partner governed action must depend on the applicable capability gate — currently
**`KORA-WP-057`** (`Certified Partner × Validated Capability × Available Capacity`) — or on `054`/`055`
directly, unless a future Founder-approved architecture explicitly supersedes this contract.

**No edge was manufactured on any existing package:** the current graph already guarantees safe ordering —
`081` ← `054`,`055`,`056`,`057`; `082` and `091` ← `081`; `103` and `105` ← `054`. This rule governs **future
package definition**, and is recorded so the guarantee is not rediscovered by accident.

### AM.2 — Partner lifecycle governance events — ownership clarification

`KORA-WP-006` owns the `audit.governance_event` substrate; `KORA-WP-058` owns the Partner Admin/governance
surface. **Clarified, creating no package and changing neither row:** Partner lifecycle transitions delivered
by `051`–`059` must emit durable governance events through the `006` substrate — admission/census, Advisor
assignment and reassignment, onboarding started and completed, certification granted or revoked, validation
granted or revoked, governed capacity/availability change, capability activation or revocation, suspension
and reactivation. **No event is prescribed for a transition that does not yet exist**; each becomes required
with the package that introduces it.

### AM.3 — Final Definition of Done

Completion is a **Product-level** condition, never "all WPs COMPLETE":

1. every intended persona has a real canonical Product experience · 2. no placeholder canonical surface ·
3. **no demo-runtime leakage** (PATCH_03 invariant provably held) · 4. no critical dead route in a primary
workflow · 5. Company lifecycle end-to-end · 6. Advisor lifecycle end-to-end **for Company and Partner** ·
7. Partner governed lifecycle end-to-end · 8. **capability-before-write enforced** (AM.1) · 9. every governed
lifecycle transition audited (AM.2) · 10. privacy/legal/external gates required for the final Product closed ·
11. Prime complete **if retained by Founder** · 12. Product Experience accepted at `131` · 13. Production
infrastructure validated · 14. **exact-SHA Production candidate validated** · 15. rollback/recovery proven ·
16. methodology-disclosure invariant maintained on every KORA Index surface · 17. **Registry execution state
mechanically derivable** (Section AL).

### AM.4 — Execution cadence

| Class | Shape | Examples |
|---|---|---|
| **SMALL batch** | 3–5 low-risk related packages; no migration, no RLS, no new authorization semantics; one combined cycle | **`136` + `137`** |
| **MEDIUM batch** | 2–3 tightly related packages sharing a module or environment; one combined staging/visual cycle where justified | `126` + `128` |
| **MEDIUM single** | no migration, but changes runtime resolution or a customer-facing surface; visual acceptance applicable | **`138`** |
| **HIGH-RISK single** | migration · RLS · new persona capability · authorization/capability semantics · money/ledger semantics · Production | **`051`**, `054`, `055`, `057`, `135`, and `100`–`109` — **never blindly batched, economic/ledger risk is per-package** |

Pre-check and governance report are produced **per batch**, not per package; exact-SHA staging validation
remains per batch; push only on explicit Founder authorization.

### AM.6 — Governance notification / tasking — **OWNED BY `KORA-WP-067`, no package created**

The Advisor/Partner lifecycle audit reported this capability as **NO OWNER**. **That finding was wrong and is
corrected here.** **`KORA-WP-067` — Notifications Minimum** already owns it: *Purpose: governance-significant
alerts only* · *Hard Deps: `KORA-WP-007`* (the Case primitive) · *Proposed New: notification-trigger
infrastructure* · *Acceptance: expiry/blocker/conflict alerts fire correctly, **nothing else does***. The
audit's keyword sweep omitted the stem "notif", so an existing I3 package was not surfaced. **No new package
was allocated, and the node/edge/status totals are unchanged.**

`067` is mechanically **READY** today — its sole Hard Dep `007` is COMPLETE (AL.2).

**Fold-in, using `067`'s own mechanism.** Its Out of Scope already states that further categories are *"folded
in once relevant"*. The Partner lifecycle hand-offs become relevant at **`054`/`055`**: Advisor assigned a
Partner · Partner onboarding requirement issued · evidence ready for review · certification/validation
hand-off · capability decision · suspension or revocation. **`067` therefore becomes required no later than
M6**, and is **not** a blocker for `051`, `052`, `053`, `056` or `058`, none of which carries a notification
requirement. **No provider is selected and no vendor/email/SMS infrastructure is in scope** — `067` owns the
governance substrate, `KORA-WP-065` owns durable job execution.

### AM.5 — External workstream register

Gate contracts are **unchanged** by this section; this is a program view of them.

| Workstream | Owner type | Becomes blocking | Product work that continues meanwhile |
|---|---|---|---|
| **`TRUST-04`** | external Legal | at `054` — **highest-leverage Partner blocker** | `051`, `052`, `053`, `056`, `058` |
